16.API API CONTRACT

GET /admin/money must return only fields permitted by the caller’s
administrative scope. Collection endpoints must expose pagination
metadata. Mutation endpoints must validate the request against the
latest resource version and must return an operation identifier when
work continues asynchronously. Errors must use KYMEX’s standard error
envelope and must not expose provider credentials or protected
implementation details.

16.TEST MATRIX

================================================================================
ADMIN CONSOLE PAGE 17: BILLING
================================================================================

The Billing page is an administrative surface for plans, prices, trials,
subscriptions, invoices, payment providers, entitlements, proration,
taxes, cancellations, and billing support. It must operate within the
selected tenant, workspace, environment, and role scope and must never
become a normal end-user navigation destination.

Use an implementation route equivalent to /admin/billing. The server
must require the dedicated permission for Billing before returning page
data. The permission check must be repeated by every mutation endpoint
used by the page. A frontend route guard is useful for UX but is never
sufficient for authorization.

17.001 Billing requirement 1: Implement server-side pagination for
potentially large collections and preserve cursor state during
navigation. For this page, apply that rule specifically to plans,
prices, trials, subscriptions, invoices, payment providers,
entitlements, proration, taxes, cancellations, and billing support,
using the selected billing context where applicable. 17.002 Billing
requirement 2: Support scoped search using normalized names,
identifiers, status values, and relevant metadata. For this page, apply
that rule specifically to plans, prices, trials, subscriptions,
invoices, payment providers, entitlements, proration, taxes,
cancellations, and billing support, using the selected billing context
where applicable. 17.003 Billing requirement 3: Allow filtering by
environment and tenant without changing the authorization context
implicitly. For this page, apply that rule specifically to plans,
prices, trials, subscriptions, invoices, payment providers,
entitlements, proration, taxes, cancellations, and billing support,
using the selected billing context where applicable. 17.004 Billing
requirement 4: Provide a clear empty state explaining what creates the
first resource without pretending that sample data is live. For this
page, apply that rule specifically to plans, prices, trials,
subscriptions, invoices, payment providers, entitlements, proration,
taxes, cancellations, and billing support, using the selected billing
context where applicable. 17.005 Billing requirement 5: Expose
provider-specific fields only after capability discovery confirms that
the provider supports them. For this page, apply that rule specifically

17.API API CONTRACT

GET /admin/billing must return only fields permitted by the caller’s
administrative scope. Collection endpoints must expose pagination
metadata. Mutation endpoints must validate the request against the
latest resource version and must return an operation identifier when
work continues asynchronously. Errors must use KYMEX’s standard error
envelope and must not expose provider credentials or protected
implementation details.

17.TEST MATRIX

================================================================================
ADMIN CONSOLE PAGE 18: VISITOR ANALYTICS
================================================================================

The Visitor Analytics page is an administrative surface for traffic,
sessions, acquisition, events, funnels, retention, privacy controls,
aggregation, dashboards, and export rules. It must operate within the
selected tenant, workspace, environment, and role scope and must never
become a normal end-user navigation destination.

Use an implementation route equivalent to /admin/visitor-analytics. The
server must require the dedicated permission for Visitor Analytics
before returning page data. The permission check must be repeated by
every mutation endpoint used by the page. A frontend route guard is
useful for UX but is never sufficient for authorization.

18.001 Visitor Analytics requirement 1: Provide a clear empty state
explaining what creates the first resource without pretending that
sample data is live. For this page, apply that rule specifically to
traffic, sessions, acquisition, events, funnels, retention, privacy
controls, aggregation, dashboards, and export rules, using the selected
visitor analytics context where applicable. 18.002 Visitor Analytics
requirement 2: Expose provider-specific fields only after capability
discovery confirms that the provider supports them. For this page, apply
that rule specifically to traffic, sessions, acquisition, events,
funnels, retention, privacy controls, aggregation, dashboards, and
export rules, using the selected visitor analytics context where
applicable. 18.003 Visitor Analytics requirement 3: Use optimistic UI
only for reversible presentation changes; authoritative backend state
remains the source of truth. For this page, apply that rule specifically
to traffic, sessions, acquisition, events, funnels, retention, privacy
controls, aggregation, dashboards, and export rules, using the selected
visitor analytics context where applicable. 18.004 Visitor Analytics
requirement 4: Handle stale data by detecting version conflicts and
asking the administrator to review the latest state. For this page,
apply that rule specifically to traffic, sessions, acquisition, events,
funnels, retention, privacy controls, aggregation, dashboards, and
export rules, using the selected visitor analytics context where
applicable. 18.005 Visitor Analytics requirement 5: Make asynchronous
work resumable where supported and expose progress without exposing
internal credentials. For this page, apply that rule specifically to
traffic, sessions, acquisition, events, funnels, retention, privacy
controls, aggregation, dashboards, and export rules, using the selected
visitor analytics context where applicable. 18.006 Visitor Analytics
requirement 6: Attach an operation ID to meaningful mutations so support
staff can correlate UI and backend records. For this page, apply that
rule specifically to traffic, sessions, acquisition, events, funnels,
retention, privacy controls, aggregation, dashboards, and export rules,
using the selected visitor analytics context where applicable. 18.007
Visitor Analytics requirement 7: Record actor, target, action, result,
timestamp, environment, and correlation identifier for auditable
mutations. For this page, apply that rule specifically to traffic,
sessions, acquisition, events, funnels, retention, privacy controls,

8: Require confirmation text or an explicit secondary confirmation for

18.API API CONTRACT

GET /admin/visitor-analytics must return only fields permitted by the
caller’s administrative scope. Collection endpoints must expose
pagination metadata. Mutation endpoints must validate the request
against the latest resource version and must return an operation
identifier when work continues asynchronously. Errors must use KYMEX’s
standard error envelope and must not expose provider credentials or
protected implementation details.

18.TEST MATRIX

================================================================================
ADMIN CONSOLE PAGE 19: EMBED
================================================================================

The Embed page is an administrative surface for embedded chat, widgets,
SDK configuration, allowed origins, appearance, behavior,
authentication, quotas, and deployment controls. It must operate within
the selected tenant, workspace, environment, and role scope and must
never become a normal end-user navigation destination.

Use an implementation route equivalent to /admin/embed. The server must
require the dedicated permission for Embed before returning page data.
The permission check must be repeated by every mutation endpoint used by
the page. A frontend route guard is useful for UX but is never
sufficient for authorization.

19.001 Embed requirement 1: Handle stale data by detecting version
conflicts and asking the administrator to review the latest state. For
this page, apply that rule specifically to embedded chat, widgets, SDK
configuration, allowed origins, appearance, behavior, authentication,
quotas, and deployment controls, using the selected embed context where
applicable. 19.002 Embed requirement 2: Make asynchronous work resumable
where supported and expose progress without exposing internal
credentials. For this page, apply that rule specifically to embedded
chat, widgets, SDK configuration, allowed origins, appearance, behavior,
authentication, quotas, and deployment controls, using the selected
embed context where applicable. 19.003 Embed requirement 3: Attach an
operation ID to meaningful mutations so support staff can correlate UI
and backend records. For this page, apply that rule specifically to
embedded chat, widgets, SDK configuration, allowed origins, appearance,
behavior, authentication, quotas, and deployment controls, using the
selected embed context where applicable. 19.004 Embed requirement 4:

deployment controls, using the selected embed context where applicable.

19.API API CONTRACT

GET /admin/embed must return only fields permitted by the caller’s
administrative scope. Collection endpoints must expose pagination
metadata. Mutation endpoints must validate the request against the
latest resource version and must return an operation identifier when
work continues asynchronously. Errors must use KYMEX’s standard error
envelope and must not expose provider credentials or protected
implementation details.

19.AUDIT EVENTS

19.TEST MATRIX

================================================================================
ADMIN CONSOLE PAGE 20: MARKETPLACE
================================================================================

The Marketplace page is an administrative surface for extensions,
agents, skills, templates, submissions, reviews, publishing, versioning,
seller controls, moderation, and distribution. It must operate within
the selected tenant, workspace, environment, and role scope and must
never become a normal end-user navigation destination.

Use an implementation route equivalent to /admin/marketplace. The server
must require the dedicated permission for Marketplace before returning
page data. The permission check must be repeated by every mutation
endpoint used by the page. A frontend route guard is useful for UX but
is never sufficient for authorization.

20.001 Marketplace requirement 1: Record actor, target, action, result,
timestamp, environment, and correlation identifier for auditable
mutations. For this page, apply that rule specifically to extensions,
agents, skills, templates, submissions, reviews, publishing, versioning,
seller controls, moderation, and distribution, using the selected
marketplace context where applicable. 20.002 Marketplace requirement 2:
Require confirmation text or an explicit secondary confirmation for
irreversible operations when appropriate. For this page, apply that rule
specifically to extensions, agents, skills, templates, submissions,
reviews, publishing, versioning, seller controls, moderation, and
distribution, using the selected marketplace context where applicable.
20.003 Marketplace requirement 3: Offer a safe preview or diff for
configuration changes that alter runtime behavior. For this page, apply
that rule specifically to extensions, agents, skills, templates,
submissions, reviews, publishing, versioning, seller controls,
moderation, and distribution, using the selected marketplace context
where applicable. 20.004 Marketplace requirement 4: Validate referenced
resources before saving relationships so broken dependencies cannot be
silently stored. For this page, apply that rule specifically to
extensions, agents, skills, templates, submissions, reviews, publishing,
versioning, seller controls, moderation, and distribution, using the
selected marketplace context where applicable. 20.005 Marketplace
requirement 5: Use transactional writes when multiple records must
change together; otherwise expose partial-failure behavior honestly. For
this page, apply that rule specifically to extensions, agents, skills,
templates, submissions, reviews, publishing, versioning, seller

20.API API CONTRACT

GET /admin/marketplace must return only fields permitted by the caller’s
administrative scope. Collection endpoints must expose pagination
metadata. Mutation endpoints must validate the request against the
latest resource version and must return an operation identifier when
work continues asynchronously. Errors must use KYMEX’s standard error
envelope and must not expose provider credentials or protected
implementation details.

20.TEST MATRIX

================================================================================
ADMIN CONSOLE PAGE 21: DOMAIN
================================================================================

The Domain page is an administrative surface for custom domains, DNS
instructions, certificates, verification, routing, redirects,
environments, status, and domain lifecycle. It must operate within the
selected tenant, workspace, environment, and role scope and must never
become a normal end-user navigation destination.

Use an implementation route equivalent to /admin/domain. The server must
require the dedicated permission for Domain before returning page data.
The permission check must be repeated by every mutation endpoint used by
the page. A frontend route guard is useful for UX but is never
sufficient for authorization.

21.001 Domain requirement 1: Validate referenced resources before saving
relationships so broken dependencies cannot be silently stored. For this
page, apply that rule specifically to custom domains, DNS instructions,
certificates, verification, routing, redirects, environments, status,
and domain lifecycle, using the selected domain context where
applicable. 21.002 Domain requirement 2: Use transactional writes when
multiple records must change together; otherwise expose partial-failure
behavior honestly. For this page, apply that rule specifically to custom
domains, DNS instructions, certificates, verification, routing,
redirects, environments, status, and domain lifecycle, using the
selected domain context where applicable. 21.003 Domain requirement 3:
Provide retry controls only for operations known to be safe to retry or
protected by idempotency. For this page, apply that rule specifically to
custom domains, DNS instructions, certificates, verification, routing,
redirects, environments, status, and domain lifecycle, using the
selected domain context where applicable. 21.004 Domain requirement 4:
Expose health diagnostics separately from remediation actions so
observing a fault cannot accidentally mutate it. For this page, apply
that rule specifically to custom domains, DNS instructions,
certificates, verification, routing, redirects, environments, status,
and domain lifecycle, using the selected domain context where
applicable. 21.005 Domain requirement 5: Keep sensitive values masked
and provide rotation or replacement flows rather than routine plaintext

21.API API CONTRACT

GET /admin/domain must return only fields permitted by the caller’s
administrative scope. Collection endpoints must expose pagination
metadata. Mutation endpoints must validate the request against the
latest resource version and must return an operation identifier when
work continues asynchronously. Errors must use KYMEX’s standard error
envelope and must not expose provider credentials or protected
implementation details.

21.DATA DATA MODEL

21.SEC SECURITY MODEL

21.UX STATES

21.AUDIT EVENTS

21.TEST MATRIX

================================================================================
ADMIN CONSOLE PAGE 22: SECRETS
================================================================================

The Secrets page is an administrative surface for secret records,
encryption, rotation, versioning, environment scoping, access grants,
provider credentials, and exposure controls. It must operate within the
selected tenant, workspace, environment, and role scope and must never
become a normal end-user navigation destination.

Use an implementation route equivalent to /admin/secrets. The server
must require the dedicated permission for Secrets before returning page
data. The permission check must be repeated by every mutation endpoint
used by the page. A frontend route guard is useful for UX but is never
sufficient for authorization.

22.001 Secrets requirement 1: Expose health diagnostics separately from
remediation actions so observing a fault cannot accidentally mutate it.
For this page, apply that rule specifically to secret records,
encryption, rotation, versioning, environment scoping, access grants,
provider credentials, and exposure controls, using the selected secrets
context where applicable. 22.002 Secrets requirement 2: Keep sensitive
values masked and provide rotation or replacement flows rather than
routine plaintext viewing. For this page, apply that rule specifically
to secret records, encryption, rotation, versioning, environment
scoping, access grants, provider credentials, and exposure controls,
using the selected secrets context where applicable. 22.003 Secrets
requirement 3: Respect environment boundaries when cloning configuration
and require explicit destination confirmation. For this page, apply that
rule specifically to secret records, encryption, rotation, versioning,
environment scoping, access grants, provider credentials, and exposure
controls, using the selected secrets context where applicable. 22.004
Secrets requirement 4: Support import/export through validated schemas
with size, type, and authorization checks. For this page, apply that
rule specifically to secret records, encryption, rotation, versioning,
environment scoping, access grants, provider credentials, and exposure
controls, using the selected secrets context where applicable. 22.005
Secrets requirement 5: Prevent unauthorized cross-tenant references when
administrators search globally. For this page, apply that rule
specifically to secret records, encryption, rotation, versioning,
environment scoping, access grants, provider credentials, and exposure
controls, using the selected secrets context where applicable. 22.006
Secrets requirement 6: Ensure mobile layouts turn dense tables into
accessible cards or horizontally scrollable regions without hiding
critical fields. For this page, apply that rule specifically to secret
records, encryption, rotation, versioning, environment scoping, access
grants, provider credentials, and exposure controls, using the selected
secrets context where applicable. 22.007 Secrets requirement 7: Provide
keyboard-accessible menus, dialogs, tabs, tables, filters, and

22.API API CONTRACT

GET /admin/secrets must return only fields permitted by the caller’s
administrative scope. Collection endpoints must expose pagination
metadata. Mutation endpoints must validate the request against the
latest resource version and must return an operation identifier when
work continues asynchronously. Errors must use KYMEX’s standard error
envelope and must not expose provider credentials or protected
implementation details.

22.DATA DATA MODEL

22.SEC SECURITY MODEL

22.UX STATES

22.AUDIT EVENTS

22.TEST MATRIX

================================================================================
ADMIN CONSOLE PAGE 23: SECURITY
================================================================================

The Security page is an administrative surface for authentication
policies, sessions, MFA, authorization, IP rules, threat controls,
security events, data protection, and incident response. It must operate
within the selected tenant, workspace, environment, and role scope and
must never become a normal end-user navigation destination.

Use an implementation route equivalent to /admin/security. The server
must require the dedicated permission for Security before returning page
data. The permission check must be repeated by every mutation endpoint
used by the page. A frontend route guard is useful for UX but is never
sufficient for authorization.

23.001 Security requirement 1: Support import/export through validated
schemas with size, type, and authorization checks. For this page, apply
that rule specifically to authentication policies, sessions, MFA,
authorization, IP rules, threat controls, security events, data
protection, and incident response, using the selected security context
where applicable. 23.002 Security requirement 2: Prevent unauthorized
cross-tenant references when administrators search globally. For this
page, apply that rule specifically to authentication policies, sessions,
MFA, authorization, IP rules, threat controls, security events, data
protection, and incident response, using the selected security context
where applicable. 23.003 Security requirement 3: Ensure mobile layouts
turn dense tables into accessible cards or horizontally scrollable

specifically to authentication policies, sessions, MFA, authorization,

incident response, using the selected security context where applicable.

23.API API CONTRACT

GET /admin/security must return only fields permitted by the caller’s
administrative scope. Collection endpoints must expose pagination
metadata. Mutation endpoints must validate the request against the
latest resource version and must return an operation identifier when
work continues asynchronously. Errors must use KYMEX’s standard error
envelope and must not expose provider credentials or protected
implementation details.

23.DATA DATA MODEL

23.SEC SECURITY MODEL

23.UX STATES

23.AUDIT EVENTS

23.TEST MATRIX

================================================================================
ADMIN CONSOLE PAGE 24: DISCOVERABILITY
================================================================================

The Discoverability page is an administrative surface for search
indexing, public metadata, robots policy, sitemap, previews, public
visibility, content controls, and discovery governance. It must operate
within the selected tenant, workspace, environment, and role scope and
must never become a normal end-user navigation destination.

Use an implementation route equivalent to /admin/discoverability. The
server must require the dedicated permission for Discoverability before
returning page data. The permission check must be repeated by every
mutation endpoint used by the page. A frontend route guard is useful for
UX but is never sufficient for authorization.

24.001 Discoverability requirement 1: Provide keyboard-accessible menus,
dialogs, tabs, tables, filters, and confirmation controls. For this
page, apply that rule specifically to search indexing, public metadata,
robots policy, sitemap, previews, public visibility, content controls,
and discovery governance, using the selected discoverability context
where applicable. 24.002 Discoverability requirement 2: Expose
machine-readable error codes internally while presenting actionable
human-readable messages in the UI. For this page, apply that rule
specifically to search indexing, public metadata, robots policy,
sitemap, previews, public visibility, content controls, and discovery
governance, using the selected discoverability context where applicable.
24.003 Discoverability requirement 3: Avoid revealing whether a
protected resource exists when the viewer lacks permission to enumerate
it. For this page, apply that rule specifically to search indexing,
public metadata, robots policy, sitemap, previews, public visibility,
content controls, and discovery governance, using the selected
discoverability context where applicable. 24.004 Discoverability
requirement 4: Cache non-sensitive metadata with explicit invalidation
rules so administrative dashboards remain fast without becoming stale
indefinitely. For this page, apply that rule specifically to search
indexing, public metadata, robots policy, sitemap, previews, public
visibility, content controls, and discovery governance, using the
selected discoverability context where applicable. 24.005
Discoverability requirement 5: Use background workers for expensive
scans, rebuilds, imports, exports, migrations, and analytics
calculations. For this page, apply that rule specifically to search
indexing, public metadata, robots policy, sitemap, previews, public
visibility, content controls, and discovery governance, using the
selected discoverability context where applicable. 24.006
Discoverability requirement 6: Show worker queue status and last
successful execution where that information is safe and relevant. For
this page, apply that rule specifically to search indexing, public
metadata, robots policy, sitemap, previews, public visibility, content

24.API API CONTRACT

GET /admin/discoverability must return only fields permitted by the
caller’s administrative scope. Collection endpoints must expose
pagination metadata. Mutation endpoints must validate the request
against the latest resource version and must return an operation
identifier when work continues asynchronously. Errors must use KYMEX’s
standard error envelope and must not expose provider credentials or
protected implementation details.

24.TEST MATRIX

================================================================================
ADMIN CONSOLE PAGE 25: ACTIVITY LOG
================================================================================

The Activity Log page is an administrative surface for immutable
administrative events, actor identity, timestamps, target resources,
before/after metadata, filtering, retention, exports, and investigation
workflows. It must operate within the selected tenant, workspace,
environment, and role scope and must never become a normal end-user
navigation destination.

Use an implementation route equivalent to /admin/activity-log. The
server must require the dedicated permission for Activity Log before
returning page data. The permission check must be repeated by every
mutation endpoint used by the page. A frontend route guard is useful for
UX but is never sufficient for authorization.

25.001 Activity Log requirement 1: Cache non-sensitive metadata with
explicit invalidation rules so administrative dashboards remain fast
without becoming stale indefinitely. For this page, apply that rule
specifically to immutable administrative events, actor identity,
timestamps, target resources, before/after metadata, filtering,
retention, exports, and investigation workflows, using the selected
activity log context where applicable. 25.002 Activity Log requirement
2: Use background workers for expensive scans, rebuilds, imports,
exports, migrations, and analytics calculations. For this page, apply

identity, timestamps, target resources, before/after metadata,
filtering, retention, exports, and investigation workflows, using the
selected activity log context where applicable. 25.003 Activity Log

actor identity, timestamps, target resources, before/after metadata,
filtering, retention, exports, and investigation workflows, using the
selected activity log context where applicable. 25.140 Activity Log
requirement 140: Keep sensitive values masked and provide rotation or
replacement flows rather than routine plaintext viewing. For this page,
apply that rule specifically to immutable administrative events, actor
identity, timestamps, target resources, before/after metadata,
filtering, retention, exports, and investigation workflows, using the
selected activity log context where applicable. 25.141 Activity Log
requirement 141: Respect environment boundaries when cloning
configuration and require explicit destination confirmation. For this
page, apply that rule specifically to immutable administrative events,
actor identity, timestamps, target resources, before/after metadata,
filtering, retention, exports, and investigation workflows, using the
selected activity log context where applicable. 25.142 Activity Log
requirement 142: Support import/export through validated schemas with
size, type, and authorization checks. For this page, apply that rule
specifically to immutable administrative events, actor identity,
timestamps, target resources, before/after metadata, filtering,
retention, exports, and investigation workflows, using the selected
activity log context where applicable. 25.143 Activity Log requirement
143: Prevent unauthorized cross-tenant references when administrators
search globally. For this page, apply that rule specifically to
immutable administrative events, actor identity, timestamps, target
resources, before/after metadata, filtering, retention, exports, and
investigation workflows, using the selected activity log context where
applicable. 25.144 Activity Log requirement 144: Ensure mobile layouts
turn dense tables into accessible cards or horizontally scrollable
regions without hiding critical fields. For this page, apply that rule
specifically to immutable administrative events, actor identity,
timestamps, target resources, before/after metadata, filtering,
retention, exports, and investigation workflows, using the selected
activity log context where applicable. 25.145 Activity Log requirement
145: Provide keyboard-accessible menus, dialogs, tabs, tables, filters,
and confirmation controls. For this page, apply that rule specifically
to immutable administrative events, actor identity, timestamps, target
resources, before/after metadata, filtering, retention, exports, and
investigation workflows, using the selected activity log context where
applicable. 25.146 Activity Log requirement 146: Expose machine-readable
error codes internally while presenting actionable human-readable
messages in the UI. For this page, apply that rule specifically to
immutable administrative events, actor identity, timestamps, target
resources, before/after metadata, filtering, retention, exports, and
investigation workflows, using the selected activity log context where
applicable. 25.147 Activity Log requirement 147: Avoid revealing whether
a protected resource exists when the viewer lacks permission to
enumerate it. For this page, apply that rule specifically to immutable
administrative events, actor identity, timestamps, target resources,
before/after metadata, filtering, retention, exports, and investigation
workflows, using the selected activity log context where applicable.
25.148 Activity Log requirement 148: Cache non-sensitive metadata with
explicit invalidation rules so administrative dashboards remain fast
without becoming stale indefinitely. For this page, apply that rule
specifically to immutable administrative events, actor identity,
timestamps, target resources, before/after metadata, filtering,
retention, exports, and investigation workflows, using the selected
activity log context where applicable. 25.149 Activity Log requirement
149: Use background workers for expensive scans, rebuilds, imports,
exports, migrations, and analytics calculations. For this page, apply
that rule specifically to immutable administrative events, actor
identity, timestamps, target resources, before/after metadata,
filtering, retention, exports, and investigation workflows, using the
selected activity log context where applicable. 25.150 Activity Log
requirement 150: Show worker queue status and last successful execution
where that information is safe and relevant. For this page, apply that
rule specifically to immutable administrative events, actor identity,
timestamps, target resources, before/after metadata, filtering,
retention, exports, and investigation workflows, using the selected
activity log context where applicable.

25.API API CONTRACT

GET /admin/activity-log must return only fields permitted by the
caller’s administrative scope. Collection endpoints must expose
pagination metadata. Mutation endpoints must validate the request
against the latest resource version and must return an operation
identifier when work continues asynchronously. Errors must use KYMEX’s
standard error envelope and must not expose provider credentials or
protected implementation details.

25.SEC SECURITY MODEL

25.UX STATES

25.AUDIT EVENTS

25.TEST MATRIX

================================================================================
ADMIN CONSOLE CROSS-PAGE ARCHITECTURE
================================================================================

ADMIN CONSOLE SHELL

The shell owns tenant selection, environment selection, administrator
identity, global search, notifications, command navigation, support
access, and session controls. It must not load every page’s sensitive
data at once. Each route fetches the minimum authorized dataset required
for its current view.

ADMIN SEARCH

Global administrative search must be permission-aware before indexing or
returning a result. Search suggestions must not reveal resource names
from scopes the actor cannot access. Results should be ranked by exact
identifier matches, normalized names, recent activity, and resource type
while preserving authorization boundaries.

COMMAND PALETTE

The command palette may expose administrative actions only after
evaluating the current actor’s permissions and selected environment.
Commands that can mutate production configuration must display the
target and require the same confirmation policy as the underlying page
action.

SESSION SECURITY

Administrative sessions should use shorter idle windows than ordinary
user sessions when appropriate to the deployment’s security policy.
Re-authentication must be possible without losing harmless navigation
context. A revoked session must invalidate subsequent privileged API
requests rather than merely hiding the console.

STEP-UP AUTHENTICATION

The platform should classify operations by impact. Read-only operational
inspection can use the normal administrative session. Credential
rotation, domain ownership changes, destructive data operations, billing
changes, production deployment, security-policy changes, and other
high-impact actions should support step-up authentication according to
configured policy.

ENVIRONMENT SAFETY

Every page must display whether the administrator is operating against
development, staging, or production. Production mutations should have
unmistakable contextual confirmation and should never be triggered
merely because the browser restored an old environment selection.

CONFIGURATION VERSIONING

Where a setting can affect runtime behavior, store a versioned
representation. The UI should permit comparison between the active
version and a proposed version, identify the actor and timestamp, and
validate dependencies before activation.

DEPENDENCY GRAPH

Administrative resources should be represented as a dependency graph.
For example, a deployed agent may depend on a skill, integration, secret
reference, model provider, storage location, and workflow. Before
disabling or deleting a dependency, calculate affected resources and
block or warn according to policy.

BACKGROUND JOBS

Expensive administrative work must execute through durable jobs. Jobs
require a stable identifier, owner scope, requested action, state
machine, progress metadata where available, start and completion
timestamps, cancellation semantics, retry policy, and final verification
result.

QUEUE FAIRNESS

Prevent one organization or one administrator from monopolizing
administrative worker capacity. Use quotas and fair scheduling
appropriate to the deployment. Critical security operations may receive
priority without allowing ordinary workloads to starve indefinitely.

PROVIDER ABSTRACTION

Provider adapters must expose capability metadata, authentication
requirements, rate limits, supported operations, idempotency behavior,
error mapping, health status, and verification methods. The Admin
Console should consume this normalized interface rather than scattering
provider-specific logic through page components.

MCP ADMINISTRATION BOUNDARY

MCP servers can be registered, inspected, authorized, enabled, disabled,
tested, and revoked from the Admin Console. Registration does not grant
every user access. Runtime access must be resolved against organization
policy, user authorization, tool scopes, and the action’s risk
classification.

MCP TOOL DISCOVERY

Do not hard-code a ceiling of 1,000 or 10,000 applications. Maintain a
provider-neutral registry capable of lazy discovery, capability
indexing, semantic search, per-tenant visibility, connection health,
multiple accounts, and dynamic tool refresh. Only the relevant tool
schemas should enter an agent’s working context.

CREDENTIAL VAULT BOUNDARY

Store credentials in a dedicated secure mechanism. The Admin Console may
manage metadata and rotation workflows but should not routinely display
secret material. Execution services receive narrowly scoped credentials
only for the operation that needs them and must prevent those
credentials from entering model prompts, chat history, source code,
screenshots, analytics, or ordinary logs.

AUDIT ARCHITECTURE

Audit records must identify actor, organization, environment, action,
target, timestamp, outcome, correlation ID, and safe metadata describing
the change. Sensitive payloads should be omitted or cryptographically
protected according to the deployment’s audit requirements. Ordinary
administrators must not be able to rewrite historical records.

OBSERVABILITY

Use separate metrics for page latency, API latency, worker latency,
provider latency, error rate, authorization denials, queue depth, cache
effectiveness, and verification failures. Administrative dashboards
should not become a side channel for secrets or private user content.

DISASTER RECOVERY

Critical administrative configuration should be backed up according to
the platform’s recovery objectives. Restores must be tested. Recovery
procedures must preserve tenant isolation, secret references, audit
integrity, and environment boundaries.

BACKUP VERIFICATION

A backup is not considered healthy merely because a job completed.
Periodically verify that representative objects can be restored and that
checksums, metadata, relationships, and access controls remain coherent.

DATA RETENTION

Every administrative data class should have a retention policy. Activity
logs, analytics, backups, configuration history, deleted resources, and
operational traces may have different retention periods. The
implementation must not silently retain sensitive data forever.

PRIVACY

Administrative analytics must use the minimum data needed for the stated
operational purpose. Where visitor analytics or user behavior is
involved, apply the platform’s privacy controls and avoid exposing
individual-level information to administrators who do not require it.

ACCESS REVIEWS

Support periodic review of administrator memberships and permissions.
Provide an auditable view of who has access to each privileged
capability and when that access was granted or changed.

BREAK-GLASS ACCESS

If the platform supports emergency administrative access, it must be
explicitly marked, time-bounded, strongly authenticated, narrowly
scoped, and heavily audited. Emergency access must not become a hidden
permanent superuser path.

CHANGE MANAGEMENT

High-impact configuration changes should support a change record
containing intent, actor, target, proposed state, approval where
required, execution result, and rollback information. This allows
support teams to reconstruct what happened without relying on chat
transcripts.

ADMIN API CONSISTENCY

All administrative endpoints should use a common envelope for success
metadata, operation identifiers, pagination, validation errors,
authorization errors, rate-limit responses, and provider failures. The
UI should not need a separate bespoke error parser for every page.

RATE LIMITING

Apply per-actor, per-organization, and per-operation limits where
appropriate. Expensive operations should be protected independently from
inexpensive reads. Rate-limit messages should remain actionable without
revealing internal capacity details that could aid abuse.

IDEMPOTENCY

Mutations such as creating resources, rotating credentials, initiating
deployments, charging money, publishing marketplace entries, or changing
domains must use provider-supported idempotency or an equivalent
server-side deduplication strategy.

TRANSACTION BOUNDARIES

When one administrative action affects multiple records, define its
transaction boundary explicitly. If an external provider cannot
participate in a database transaction, persist a durable operation state
and reconciliation strategy instead of pretending that local and remote
state changed atomically.

RECONCILIATION

Background reconciliation should compare authoritative provider state
with KYMEX records for integrations, subscriptions, deployments,
domains, storage, and other externally managed resources. Differences
should become visible incidents or actionable drift records rather than
silent inconsistencies.

ADMIN NOTIFICATIONS

Notify authorized administrators about failed critical jobs, security
events, expiring certificates, integration failures, billing anomalies,
storage pressure, and deployment failures according to configured
notification policies. Do not include raw credentials or unnecessarily
sensitive user content.

SUPPORT DIAGNOSTICS

Provide a safe diagnostic bundle containing operation IDs, timestamps,
error classes, provider request IDs, relevant configuration versions,
and non-sensitive environment metadata. Never create a diagnostic export
that automatically dumps secrets.

MOBILE ADMINISTRATION

Mobile access must not simply shrink desktop tables. Prioritize critical
state, provide expandable details, preserve confirmation context, and
ensure dangerous actions cannot be triggered accidentally by touch.
Sensitive screens should respect device-level privacy considerations.

ACCESSIBILITY

Administrative controls must have semantic labels, visible focus,
logical tab order, keyboard-operable menus, accessible dialog behavior,
screen-reader announcements for asynchronous state, and sufficient
non-color indicators. The Blue/Green/Gray design system must never be
the only way to communicate status.

PERFORMANCE

Use route-level code splitting for the Admin Console, server-side
pagination, selective data loading, cache invalidation, debounced
search, and background workers for heavy operations. Do not preload
sensitive datasets simply to make navigation appear faster.

SECURITY TESTING

Test tenant isolation, role escalation, IDOR-style resource access,
stale-session reuse, revoked-token behavior, CSRF protections where
relevant, secret leakage, log leakage, unsafe redirects, untrusted
provider output, prompt injection through external content, and mutation
replay.

RELEASE GATES

No Admin Console release is complete until route authorization, API
authorization, audit events, high-risk confirmation, error handling,
mobile accessibility, provider failure behavior, and truthful
post-action verification pass automated and manual acceptance tests.

PART 9: KYMEX AI — UNIVERSAL COMPUTER-USE, BROWSER AUTOMATION, MCP APP
ORCHESTRATION, CREDENTIAL-AWARE ACTIONS, SCREENSHOT, AND TOOL DISCOVERY
================================================================================

IMPORTANT IMPLEMENTATION DIRECTIVE

This section is ADDITIVE ONLY.

Do NOT replace, summarize, condense, rewrite, delete, reorder, or weaken
any previous section of the KYMEX AI Prompts. Preserve all previously
specified product capabilities, pages, components, workflows, security
requirements, providers, models, agents, builders, media tools,
integrations, and Control Center functionality.

This section adds a universal action layer that turns the KYMEX AI Agent
/ Chatbot into an intelligent computer-use and application-orchestration
agent.

The goal is not merely to create a chatbot that can describe how to
perform actions. The goal is to create an agent that can, when the user
authorizes it and the connected service permits it, actually perform
supported actions, observe their results, recover from routine failures,
and report truthfully what happened.

The agent must be SMART, FAST, INTELLIGENT, EFFECTIVE, CONTEXT-AWARE,
CAPABILITY-AWARE, SECURITY-AWARE, and ACTION-ORIENTED.

The agent must not claim to have clicked, logged in, uploaded,
downloaded, created, changed, sent, published, deployed, purchased,
booked, synchronized, or otherwise completed an action unless the
underlying execution layer actually confirms that action.

  --------------------------------
  9.1 UNIVERSAL ACTION PRINCIPLE
  --------------------------------

9.1.001 The main KYMEX AI Agent must support conversational requests and
action requests through the same intelligent interface.

9.1.002 The user must be able to say things such as: “Open this link.”
“Click the pricing button.” “Take a screenshot.” “Send me a screenshot
of the current page.” “Go through this website.” “Log into my account
using the credentials I provided.” “Connect my Google account.” “Connect
Base44.” “Connect Lovable.” “Open my Google Calendar.” “Create a
calendar event.” “Use my connected GitHub.” “Inspect this website.”
“Build this in Base44.” “Continue the coding task in the connected
coding environment.” “Check this website and tell me what happens.”
“Download the report.” “Upload this file.” “Fill out the form.” “Use the
authorized account.” “Complete the workflow.” “Show me exactly what the
website looks like.” “Click every relevant link and investigate.” “Use
the best available connected tool.” “Find the right MCP app
automatically.”

9.1.003 The agent must convert natural-language requests into an
executable plan when the request requires multiple actions.

9.1.004 The plan must be generated dynamically from the user’s intent,
available tools, connected integrations, permissions, current
application state, and task constraints.

9.1.005 The agent must not require the user to know which tool, MCP
server, browser action, API, model, or provider is needed.

9.1.006 The user should describe the outcome; KYMEX AI should determine
the appropriate execution mechanism.

9.1.007 If several execution mechanisms are available, the orchestration
engine must select the most appropriate one using capability,
authorization, latency, reliability, cost, data locality, and task
requirements.

9.1.008 Tool selection must be dynamic.

9.1.009 The system must not hard-code a small finite list of
applications as the only supported integrations.

9.1.010 The architecture must support discovery and registration of new
MCP-compatible applications without requiring the core agent to be
rewritten for every newly available application.

9.1.011 The system must be capable of supporting a very large and
continuously changing ecosystem of MCP servers, connectors, tools, and
applications.

9.1.012 The prompt must explicitly state that the examples of
applications listed elsewhere are examples, not the complete integration
universe.

9.1.013 The agent must be designed for thousands to tens of thousands of
potential connected applications and tools.

9.1.014 The agent must discover capabilities from provider metadata and
connection state instead of assuming capabilities from an application
name.

9.1.015 The agent must distinguish between: - an application being
known, - an application being discoverable, - an application being
connected, - an application being authorized, - a tool being
available, - a tool being permitted, - and an action actually
succeeding.

9.1.016 These states must never be conflated.

  ------------------------------------------
  9.2 UNIVERSAL TOOL / CAPABILITY REGISTRY
  ------------------------------------------

9.2.001 Create a Universal Capability Registry.

9.2.002 The registry must maintain normalized metadata for all tools
available to KYMEX AI.

9.2.003 A capability record should contain, where available: - provider
ID - application ID - MCP server ID - tool ID - tool name -
human-readable description - input schema - output schema -
authorization requirements - required scopes - risk classification -
supported environments - supported regions where relevant - latency
estimate - reliability metrics - pricing/credit characteristics - rate
limits - supported media types - supported file types - supported
actions - read/write classification - destructive-action
classification - approval requirements - authentication state -
connection state - last health check - version - compatibility
information - provenance - documentation reference

9.2.004 The registry must support capability search.

9.2.005 Capability search must be semantic, not only keyword-based.

9.2.006 The registry must support filtering by action.

9.2.007 Example action filters: - read - search - create - update -
delete - upload - download - send - publish - deploy - schedule -
analyze - generate - transform - browse - click - screenshot - code -
database - messaging - calendar - payments - media - automation

9.2.008 The registry must support application discovery without
requiring the user to browse thousands of entries manually.

9.2.009 The registry must support fuzzy application-name matching.

9.2.010 The registry must support natural-language capability matching.

9.2.011 If the user asks for an operation and no exact application name
is given, the system must search for a suitable connected capability.

9.2.012 If the user explicitly names an application, prioritize that
application when its capabilities match the task.

9.2.013 If the named application is not connected, show the connection
path when one exists.

9.2.014 If the named application has no suitable capability, explain the
limitation and search for an authorized alternative when the user
permits alternative providers.

9.2.015 Never silently substitute a materially different service for an
explicitly requested service when doing so could change the user’s
result.

  -------------------------------------------------------
  9.3 MCP-FIRST EXTENSIBLE APPLICATION CONNECTION LAYER
  -------------------------------------------------------

9.3.001 Build a first-class MCP Connection Manager.

9.3.002 MCP must be treated as a major extensibility mechanism rather
than a hard-coded integration list.

9.3.003 The connection manager must support discovering MCP servers.

9.3.004 The connection manager must support adding an MCP server.

9.3.005 The connection manager must support authorizing an MCP server.

9.3.006 The connection manager must support testing an MCP server.

9.3.007 The connection manager must support listing available tools.

9.3.008 The connection manager must support listing available resources.

9.3.009 The connection manager must support listing available prompts
where supported.

9.3.010 The connection manager must support capability refresh.

9.3.011 The connection manager must support disconnect.

9.3.012 The connection manager must support reconnect.

9.3.013 The connection manager must support revoking authorization.

9.3.014 The connection manager must support per-project connection
scope.

9.3.015 The connection manager must support per-workspace connection
scope.

9.3.016 The connection manager must support per-user connection scope.

9.3.017 The connection manager must support organization-level
connections where authorized.

9.3.018 The connection manager must support environment-specific
connections.

9.3.019 Development connections must be separable from staging
connections.

9.3.020 Staging connections must be separable from production
connections.

9.3.021 Connection metadata must not expose access-token values to
ordinary UI components.

9.3.022 Secrets must be stored through the secure secrets architecture
already defined by KYMEX AI.

9.3.023 MCP authorization must use the authorization mechanism
appropriate to the MCP server.

9.3.024 For HTTP-based MCP authorization, implement standards-compatible
authorization flows and secure token handling.

9.3.025 The current MCP authorization specification describes
OAuth-based authorization, protected-resource metadata discovery,
resource indicators, and audience-bound access tokens. The KYMEX
implementation must follow the applicable current MCP specification
rather than inventing a proprietary authentication flow.

9.3.026 The MCP client must request only the scopes necessary for the
intended operation.

9.3.027 Additional scopes must be requested only when required.

9.3.028 The UI must clearly explain newly requested permissions.

9.3.029 The user must be able to cancel authorization.

9.3.030 A failed authorization must not be represented as a successful
connection.

9.3.031 Expired authorization must transition to an explicit
reauthorization state.

9.3.032 Revoked authorization must transition to a disconnected or
revoked state.

9.3.033 The agent must not repeatedly request authorization in a tight
loop.

9.3.034 Authorization failures must be recoverable.

9.3.035 The agent must not attempt to bypass an application’s
authorization screen.

9.3.036 The agent must not obtain credentials by scraping another
application’s private storage.

9.3.037 The agent must use supported authorization mechanisms.

9.3.038 MCP servers and clients must validate tokens and intended
audiences according to the applicable MCP authorization specification.

9.3.039 Tokens must not be placed in URLs.

9.3.040 Tokens must not be written to ordinary logs.

9.3.041 Upstream tokens must not be blindly passed through to unrelated
downstream services.

9.3.042 The connection manager must maintain an audit trail of
connection, authorization, scope-change, disconnection, and revocation
events.

  -------------------------------
  9.4 DYNAMIC MCP APP DISCOVERY
  -------------------------------

9.4.001 Do not enumerate a fixed list of “supported MCP apps” in the
master prompt.

9.4.002 Instead define an open-ended MCP discovery architecture.

9.4.003 The system must be able to discover newly registered MCP
applications when they are available through configured registries,
catalogs, enterprise registries, direct server URLs, or other supported
discovery mechanisms.

9.4.004 The system must not assume that the examples Google, Base44,
Lovable, Google Calendar, Claude, GitHub, or any other named service
represent the entire ecosystem.

9.4.005 The architecture must accommodate thousands or tens of thousands
of MCP-compatible services without changing the agent’s fundamental
reasoning architecture.

9.4.006 Use lazy discovery rather than loading every tool definition
into every model context.

9.4.007 Maintain compact capability indexes.

9.4.008 Retrieve detailed tool schemas only when a candidate tool is
selected.

9.4.009 Cache stable metadata with appropriate invalidation.

9.4.010 Refresh tool metadata after server version changes.

9.4.011 Refresh metadata after connection changes.

9.4.012 Refresh metadata after permission changes.

9.4.013 Support semantic routing over the capability registry.

9.4.014 Support application aliases.

9.4.015 Support provider aliases.

9.4.016 Support user-defined names for connections.

9.4.017 Example: The user may name a connection “My Work Google”.

9.4.018 The agent must resolve that alias to the correct connection
without confusing it with another Google connection.

9.4.019 Support multiple accounts for the same provider.

9.4.020 Example: The user may have personal Google, work Google,
multiple GitHub accounts, multiple calendars, multiple cloud-storage
accounts, and multiple builder accounts.

9.4.021 The agent must ask which account to use only when the correct
account cannot be determined safely from context and connection
metadata.

9.4.022 The agent must never silently use the wrong account.

  ----------------------------------
  9.5 BROWSER / COMPUTER-USE AGENT
  ----------------------------------

9.5.001 Create a first-class Browser Agent.

9.5.002 The Browser Agent must be capable of navigating authorized
websites.

9.5.003 The Browser Agent must be able to open URLs.

9.5.004 The Browser Agent must be able to follow links.

9.5.005 The Browser Agent must be able to click buttons.

9.5.006 The Browser Agent must be able to click links.

9.5.007 The Browser Agent must be able to select menu items.

9.5.008 The Browser Agent must be able to scroll.

9.5.009 The Browser Agent must be able to search within pages.

9.5.010 The Browser Agent must be able to type into form fields.

9.5.011 The Browser Agent must be able to select dropdown values.

9.5.012 The Browser Agent must be able to check and uncheck supported
controls.

9.5.013 The Browser Agent must be able to upload files when the user has
authorized the upload.

9.5.014 The Browser Agent must be able to download files when the user
has authorized the download.

9.5.015 The Browser Agent must be able to navigate back and forward.

9.5.016 The Browser Agent must be able to open a new tab when required.

9.5.017 The Browser Agent must be able to switch tabs.

9.5.018 The Browser Agent must maintain a structured representation of
active tabs.

9.5.019 The Browser Agent must identify the current URL.

9.5.020 The Browser Agent must identify the page title.

9.5.021 The Browser Agent must inspect visible page text.

9.5.022 The Browser Agent must inspect accessible UI elements.

9.5.023 The Browser Agent must use DOM/accessibility information when
available rather than relying solely on pixels.

9.5.024 The Browser Agent must use visual perception when DOM or
accessibility information is insufficient.

9.5.025 The Browser Agent must reason over screenshots when required.

9.5.026 The Browser Agent must detect page transitions.

9.5.027 The Browser Agent must detect loading states.

9.5.028 The Browser Agent must detect error pages.

9.5.029 The Browser Agent must detect login pages.

9.5.030 The Browser Agent must detect consent pages.

9.5.031 The Browser Agent must detect CAPTCHA challenges.

9.5.032 CAPTCHA must not be bypassed through prohibited mechanisms.

9.5.033 When a CAPTCHA requires human interaction, pause and request the
user to complete the necessary interaction when appropriate.

9.5.034 The Browser Agent must detect whether an action actually
succeeded.

9.5.035 The Browser Agent must verify post-action state.

9.5.036 Example: After clicking “Save”, do not report “Saved” merely
because the click event was issued. Verify the resulting state.

9.5.037 Example: After clicking “Download”, verify that the download
actually began or that the expected file was produced.

9.5.038 Example: After submitting a form, verify the resulting success
state, confirmation, or server response.

9.5.039 Example: After clicking a navigation link, verify the
destination page.

9.5.040 Browser operations must support retry when the failure is
transient.

9.5.041 Browser operations must use bounded retries.

9.5.042 Browser operations must avoid accidental repeated destructive
actions.

9.5.043 Before repeating a consequential action, inspect whether the
first attempt may already have succeeded.

9.5.044 Browser actions must support cancellation.

9.5.045 Browser actions must support timeout.

9.5.046 Browser actions must support resumable long-running sessions
where technically appropriate.

9.5.047 Browser sessions must be isolated per task.

9.5.048 Browser sessions must not leak cookies between unrelated users.

9.5.049 Browser sessions must not leak cookies between unrelated
projects.

9.5.050 Browser sessions must use secure credential storage.

  -----------------------------
  9.6 CLICK-LINK INTELLIGENCE
  -----------------------------

9.6.001 The user must be able to provide a URL and ask KYMEX AI to
inspect it.

9.6.002 The user must be able to ask KYMEX AI to click a particular
link.

9.6.003 The user must be able to describe a link semantically.

9.6.004 Example: “Click the Sign Up link.”

9.6.005 Example: “Open the pricing page.”

9.6.006 Example: “Click the button that says Get Started.”

9.6.007 Example: “Open the documentation link.”

9.6.008 Example: “Follow the link to the API reference.”

9.6.009 The agent must map the request to an appropriate interactive
element.

9.6.010 The agent must prefer exact text matches when available.

9.6.011 The agent must use accessible names when available.

9.6.012 The agent may use semantic similarity when exact text is
unavailable.

9.6.013 The agent may use visual location when required.

9.6.014 Before clicking an ambiguous element, the agent should resolve
ambiguity through context or ask the user when multiple materially
different targets exist.

9.6.015 The agent must not click arbitrary unrelated links merely
because they are visually prominent.

9.6.016 The agent must identify the target before consequential clicks.

9.6.017 The agent must detect whether a link opens a new tab.

9.6.018 The agent must detect whether a link triggers a download.

9.6.019 The agent must detect whether a link opens a modal.

9.6.020 The agent must detect whether a link changes application state.

9.6.021 The agent must record navigation history within the task.

  -------------------------------
  9.7 WEBSITE SCREENSHOT SYSTEM
  -------------------------------

9.7.001 Create a first-class Screenshot Service.

9.7.002 The Browser Agent must be able to capture screenshots of
authorized web pages.

9.7.003 The user must be able to explicitly request a screenshot.

9.7.004 Example: “Send me a screenshot of the website.”

9.7.005 Example: “Screenshot the current page.”

9.7.006 Example: “Show me what the homepage looks like.”

9.7.007 Example: “Take a screenshot after clicking Pricing.”

9.7.008 Example: “Send screenshots of every important page you visit.”

9.7.009 Example: “Screenshot the error message.”

9.7.010 Example: “Take a full-page screenshot.”

9.7.011 Support viewport screenshots.

9.7.012 Support full-page screenshots where technically possible.

9.7.013 Support element screenshots where technically possible.

9.7.014 Support screenshot after a specific action.

9.7.015 Support screenshot before an action.

9.7.016 Support screenshot after a page transition.

9.7.017 Support screenshot of a particular tab.

9.7.018 Support screenshot of the browser viewport.

9.7.019 Support screenshot of the rendered website rather than a textual
description alone.

9.7.020 Attach screenshots directly to the chat response when the user
requests them and the platform supports image attachments.

9.7.021 Store screenshots temporarily or in project storage only
according to the task and retention policy.

9.7.022 Give screenshots provenance metadata internally: - task ID -
URL - timestamp - viewport - page title - browser session ID -
screenshot type

9.7.023 Do not expose sensitive browser-session identifiers to the user
unless needed.

9.7.024 Do not accidentally include unrelated tabs in a screenshot.

9.7.025 Do not expose credentials, authentication tokens, secret keys,
or private browser UI in screenshots.

9.7.026 If a page contains highly sensitive information, apply the
configured privacy handling rules before storage or transmission.

9.7.027 Do not falsely claim that a screenshot represents a page state
that was not actually captured.

9.7.028 If screenshot capture fails, report the failure accurately and
provide a textual alternative only if useful.

9.7.029 Support screenshot comparison.

9.7.030 Support before-and-after screenshots.

9.7.031 Support annotated screenshots when the user requests
annotations.

9.7.032 Support screenshot galleries for multi-page inspections.

  ---------------------------------
  9.8 AUTHORIZED USER CREDENTIALS
  ---------------------------------

9.8.001 The agent must support authorized account access when the
connected service and security architecture permit it.

9.8.002 The user may provide credentials through a secure credential
interface or a supported authentication flow.

9.8.003 Never treat credentials typed into ordinary chat text as a
reason to store them permanently.

9.8.004 Prefer OAuth, passkeys, delegated authorization, secure
credential vaults, or provider-native login flows over copying raw
passwords into agent memory.

9.8.005 If a website requires direct credential entry and the browser
agent supports secure credential injection, credentials must be injected
only into the intended domain and intended fields.

9.8.006 Credentials must never be exposed to the model unnecessarily.

9.8.007 The model should receive only a capability such as “authorized
login available” rather than the raw password whenever possible.

9.8.008 The browser automation layer must determine the destination
domain before injecting credentials.

9.8.009 Do not inject credentials into an unexpected domain.

9.8.010 Do not inject credentials into a page whose identity cannot be
verified according to the configured security policy.

9.8.011 Detect suspicious redirects before credential submission.

9.8.012 Detect lookalike domains where security tooling permits.

9.8.013 Require user confirmation when a login action has unusual risk
or cannot be confidently associated with the intended service.

9.8.014 Do not save passwords into ordinary project files.

9.8.015 Do not put passwords into generated source code.

9.8.016 Do not put passwords into workflow definitions.

9.8.017 Do not put passwords into agent prompts.

9.8.018 Do not put passwords into analytics events.

9.8.019 Do not put passwords into activity logs.

9.8.020 Redact credentials from screenshots.

9.8.021 Redact credentials from browser traces where technically
possible.

9.8.022 Redact credentials from error messages.

9.8.023 Support credential expiration.

9.8.024 Support credential rotation.

9.8.025 Support revocation.

9.8.026 Support per-site credential isolation.

9.8.027 Support per-user credential isolation.

9.8.028 Support per-project credential isolation.

9.8.029 Support organization credential policies.

9.8.030 Never infer authorization from the mere fact that a credential
exists.

9.8.031 The user must have authorized the intended action.

9.8.032 Authorization may be persistent for low-risk operations when
explicitly configured.

9.8.033 High-risk operations may require just-in-time confirmation.

9.8.034 Examples of potentially high-risk operations include financial
actions, account deletion, security-setting changes, publication of
sensitive content, or irreversible destructive actions.

9.8.035 The policy engine must classify action risk rather than relying
only on tool names.

  --------------------------------------------
  9.9 LOGIN AND AUTHENTICATION STATE MACHINE
  --------------------------------------------

9.9.001 Implement a standardized login state machine.

9.9.002 States: - NOT_REQUIRED - REQUIRED - AUTHORIZATION_PENDING -
LOGIN_PAGE_DETECTED - CREDENTIALS_AVAILABLE -
CREDENTIAL_INJECTION_PENDING - USER_INTERACTION_REQUIRED -
MFA_REQUIRED - CAPTCHA_REQUIRED - AUTHENTICATING - AUTHENTICATED -
AUTHENTICATION_FAILED - SESSION_EXPIRED - SESSION_REVOKED -
SECURITY_BLOCKED

9.9.003 The agent must transition between states based on observed
evidence.

9.9.004 The agent must not assume authentication succeeded because
credentials were submitted.

9.9.005 Verify the authenticated landing state.

9.9.006 Detect MFA.

9.9.007 When MFA requires user interaction, pause safely.

9.9.008 Never ask the user to send a one-time authentication code into
an unprotected log if the provider supplies a secure approval mechanism.

9.9.009 Resume after successful user completion when the browser session
remains valid.

9.9.010 Detect expired sessions.

9.9.011 Re-authenticate only through authorized mechanisms.

9.9.012 Avoid infinite re-login loops.

  ----------------------------------
  9.10 ACTION AUTHORIZATION ENGINE
  ----------------------------------

9.10.001 Create a central Action Authorization Engine.

9.10.002 Every external action must pass through the engine.

9.10.003 The engine must evaluate: - user authorization - project
permissions - workspace permissions - organization policy - integration
scope - tool scope - resource ownership - action risk - approval
requirements - environment - target domain - spending limits - rate
limits - policy restrictions - provider requirements

9.10.004 The engine must support: - automatically allowed actions -
user-confirmed actions - administrator-approved actions - blocked
actions

9.10.005 Low-risk read operations may execute automatically when
authorized.

9.10.006 Routine navigation may execute automatically when requested.

9.10.007 Consequential writes must follow the configured approval
policy.

9.10.008 Destructive operations must require appropriate confirmation.

9.10.009 Financial transactions must follow explicit transaction
authorization.

9.10.010 Account-security changes must follow elevated authorization.

9.10.011 The agent must never bypass authorization because the user is
in a hurry.

9.10.012 The agent must never bypass a provider’s access control.

  ------------------------------
  9.11 MULTI-APP ORCHESTRATION
  ------------------------------

9.11.001 The agent must be able to combine multiple connected
applications within a single task.

9.11.002 Example: Use Google Drive to retrieve a file, analyze it, use a
connected builder to create a website, use a Git provider to commit
code, use an image generator to create assets, use a video generator to
create a demo, and then deploy through an authorized deployment service.

9.11.003 Each action must be executed through the appropriate
capability.

9.11.004 The agent must maintain a shared task context.

9.11.005 The agent must maintain a structured artifact registry.

9.11.006 The artifact registry must track: - artifact ID - type -
source - provider - project - version - location - permissions -
creation time - dependencies - provenance

9.11.007 The agent must pass only the required data between
applications.

9.11.008 Avoid unnecessarily copying private data between providers.

9.11.009 Prefer references or secure transfer mechanisms when supported.

9.11.010 Track cross-application dependencies.

9.11.011 Detect when one provider’s output is incompatible with another
provider’s input.

9.11.012 Transform data through controlled adapters.

9.11.013 Validate transformed output before passing it onward.

  --------------------------------------------
  9.12 EXAMPLES OF DYNAMIC APP ORCHESTRATION
  --------------------------------------------

9.12.001 Google-class services may be used for mail, drive, calendar,
docs, sheets, contacts, maps, or other capabilities when the connected
MCP/app provides them.

9.12.002 Base44-class builders may be used for application generation,
iteration, data models, deployment, or project operations when the
connected capability exposes those actions.

9.12.003 Lovable-class builders may be used for application generation,
iteration, code changes, or project operations when the connected
capability exposes those actions.

9.12.004 Claude-class AI services may be used as a model or connected
capability where the integration provides that functionality.

9.12.005 GitHub-class services may be used for repositories, commits,
issues, pull requests, actions, or other authorized operations.

9.12.006 Calendar services may be used for scheduling and event
management.

9.12.007 Image-generation services may be used for image creation and
editing.

9.12.008 Video-generation services may be used for video creation and
editing.

9.12.009 Avatar-generation services may be used for presenter or avatar
content when connected and authorized.

9.12.010 Coding-agent services may be used for repository-level software
engineering when connected and authorized.

9.12.011 Browser agents may be used when no structured API or MCP
capability exists and browser interaction is appropriate.

9.12.012 These examples must never become a hard-coded ceiling on the
product.

  ---------------------------------
  9.13 INTELLIGENT ROUTING ENGINE
  ---------------------------------

9.13.001 Create a Universal Routing Engine.

9.13.002 The routing engine must select among: - direct internal tools -
MCP tools - browser automation - API integrations - coding agents -
research tools - model providers - media providers - workflow engines -
data-analysis tools

9.13.003 Route using task intent.

9.13.004 Route using required capabilities.

9.13.005 Route using authorization.

9.13.006 Route using latency.

9.13.007 Route using reliability.

9.13.008 Route using cost.

9.13.009 Route using quality requirements.

9.13.010 Route using data sensitivity.

9.13.011 Route using output format.

9.13.012 Route using provider availability.

9.13.013 Route using user preferences when configured.

9.13.014 Route using organization policies.

9.13.015 Route using current task state.

9.13.016 Avoid unnecessary tool calls.

9.13.017 Avoid unnecessary model calls.

9.13.018 Avoid unnecessary browser navigation.

9.13.019 Cache safe intermediate results.

9.13.020 Reuse already-obtained page state when valid.

9.13.021 Reuse authenticated sessions only when authorization permits.

9.13.022 Do not reuse stale state for consequential actions.

  -----------------------------------------
  9.14 FAST PATH / SMART PATH / DEEP PATH
  -----------------------------------------

9.14.001 Implement three execution modes internally.

9.14.002 FAST PATH: Use when the task is straightforward and a direct
capability is available.

9.14.003 SMART PATH: Use when several tools exist and lightweight
planning is required.

9.14.004 DEEP PATH: Use for complex multi-step tasks, ambiguous
environments, coding tasks, multi-application workflows, research, or
tasks requiring verification.

9.14.005 The system should automatically choose the appropriate path.

9.14.006 Do not force users to select technical execution modes.

9.14.007 The agent may expose execution progress without exposing hidden
reasoning or chain-of-thought.

9.14.008 Show concise action summaries such as: “Opening the page.”
“Checking the available tools.” “Connecting to your authorized Google
account.” “Taking the screenshot.” “Uploading the file.” “Verifying the
result.”

9.14.009 Do not expose private chain-of-thought.

  -------------------
  9.15 TASK PLANNER
  -------------------

9.15.001 Build a structured task planner.

9.15.002 Represent a task as a directed execution graph.

9.15.003 Each step must contain: - action - tool - input -
dependencies - expected output - authorization requirement - risk -
timeout - retry policy - verification rule - rollback strategy where
applicable

9.15.004 Steps may execute sequentially.

9.15.005 Independent read-only steps may execute in parallel.

9.15.006 Do not execute conflicting writes in parallel unless the system
explicitly supports transactional coordination.

9.15.007 The planner must re-plan after unexpected state changes.

9.15.008 The planner must stop if a security boundary is encountered.

9.15.009 The planner must request user input when a required decision
cannot be inferred safely.

9.15.010 The planner must preserve completed work when a later step
fails.

9.15.011 The planner must expose a task timeline.

9.15.012 The user may inspect the action timeline.

9.15.013 The user may cancel the task.

9.15.014 Cancellation must propagate to active jobs where possible.

  ------------------------------------
  9.16 BROWSER + MCP HYBRID STRATEGY
  ------------------------------------

9.16.001 Prefer structured APIs or MCP tools over visual browser
automation when they provide the exact required operation safely.

9.16.002 Use browser automation when: - no structured capability
exists, - the user explicitly requests website inspection, - visual
verification is required, - the site exposes functionality only through
its UI, - or browser interaction is itself the requested task.

9.16.003 Use MCP plus browser automation when both are useful.

9.16.004 Example: Use a calendar MCP tool to create an event and browser
automation to inspect the resulting web UI if the user requests a visual
confirmation.

9.16.005 Example: Use a builder MCP capability to modify an app and
browser automation to inspect the deployed result.

9.16.006 Example: Use Git MCP to commit code and browser automation to
inspect the repository web interface when requested.

9.16.007 The agent must not perform redundant actions through both
mechanisms unless verification requires it.

  ------------------------------
  9.17 WEBSITE INSPECTION MODE
  ------------------------------

9.17.001 Create a Website Inspection Mode.

9.17.002 User can provide a URL.

9.17.003 The agent opens the URL.

9.17.004 The agent records the initial page title and URL.

9.17.005 The agent captures an initial screenshot when requested.

9.17.006 The agent identifies major navigation elements.

9.17.007 The agent identifies forms.

9.17.008 The agent identifies calls to action.

9.17.009 The agent identifies links.

9.17.010 The agent identifies errors.

9.17.011 The agent identifies visible authentication requirements.

9.17.012 The agent can follow selected links.

9.17.013 The agent can return to the previous page.

9.17.014 The agent can create a screenshot sequence.

9.17.015 The agent can summarize visited pages after actual inspection.

9.17.016 The agent must distinguish observed facts from inferred
behavior.

9.17.017 The agent must not claim to have inspected pages it did not
load.

  ---------------------------------------------
  9.18 REQUESTED SCREENSHOT RESPONSE CONTRACT
  ---------------------------------------------

9.18.001 If the user requests a screenshot and the page is available,
provide the actual captured screenshot.

9.18.002 Do not replace the screenshot with a textual description when
an actual screenshot can be supplied.

9.18.003 If the user requests multiple screenshots, provide all
requested screenshots or clearly report which ones could not be
captured.

9.18.004 Include a concise label identifying each screenshot.

9.18.005 Example: “Homepage — captured after navigation.”

9.18.006 Example: “Pricing page — captured after clicking Pricing.”

9.18.007 Do not include raw authentication cookies or tokens in
screenshot metadata visible to the user.

9.18.008 Preserve screenshot quality sufficient for UI inspection.

  ----------------------------------------
  9.19 USER-PROVIDED CREDENTIAL WORKFLOW
  ----------------------------------------

9.19.001 Provide a secure “Authorized Account Access” control.

9.19.002 The UI must clearly distinguish: - connect account - use
existing connected account - enter credentials for one-time use -
authorize an OAuth application - approve a browser session

9.19.003 The system must prefer “Connect account” over asking the user
to paste passwords into chat.

9.19.004 If the user explicitly chooses a one-time credential workflow
and the environment supports secure credential entry, route the
credential through the secure browser/credential layer.

9.19.005 Never persist one-time credentials unless the user explicitly
opts into secure storage and the architecture supports it.

9.19.006 Never expose credentials to unrelated tools.

9.19.007 Never forward credentials to an MCP server unless the server’s
supported authorization model explicitly requires it and the user has
authorized that connection.

9.19.008 Never place credentials into an LLM prompt.

9.19.009 Never include credentials in model context unnecessarily.

9.19.010 Never include credentials in generated code.

  -------------------------------
